Chapter 14.25: Transmission & 
Posting of Unofficial Results
The results that are reported on Election Night are unofficial results. Election 
results do not become official until they are certified by the highest level of 
canvass applicable to the contest. However, unofficial results reporting is one 
of the most important Election Night responsibilities because in almost all 
circumstances, the unofficial results will reflect the winners of the day’s contests. 
Voters will want to know the results of the election in which they cast their 
ballots by the time they go to bed on Election Night. Posting unofficial results in 
a timely manner is not only required by law, but it also maintains integrity in the 
election system, and reduces suspicion and anxiety.
Sec. 14.25.1: Step 1: Chief 
Inspector Reports Unofficial 
Results to Municipal Clerk
The Chief Inspector, or one of the poll workers appointed by him or her, 
immediately after the votes are tabulated or counted at each election, shall 
report the returns of the election to the municipal clerk or to the school district 
clerk for school district elections, except in 1st class cities. Wis. Stat. § 7.51(4)(b). 
The method of reporting results depends on the municipal clerk’s preference.
Some clerks authorize more than one transmission method:
 Ɋ By USB or other removable memory drive
 Ɋ By hand delivery of results tape or tally sheet
 Ɋ By phone
Note: For jurisdictions that modem their results directly to the county 
clerk’s election management software, separate reporting to the 
municipal clerk may not be necessary, though poll workers should 
follow their municipal clerk’s preference.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 235
Sec. 14.25.2: Step 2: Municipal Clerk 
Proofs Returns before Posting & 
Sending to County Clerk
Best Practice: Before unofficial results are made public, more than one 
person from the municipal clerk’s staff should proof the information 
to ensure publicized returns, though unofficial, accurately and 
completely reflect the returns as received from the polling places.
What to check:
 Ɋ Are all candidates included?
 Ɋ Are the correct ward(s) represented?
 Ɋ Are the transposed numbers the same?
If there are questions about the unofficial results received from a polling place, 
the municipal clerk must contact the Chief Inspector of that polling place for 
more information.
Sec. 14.25.3: Step 3: Municipal Clerk 
Makes Unofficial Results Public 
along with Number of Provisional 
Voters & Absentee Voters
The municipal clerk, or school district clerk, shall then make the returns public. 
Wis. Stat. § 7.51(4)(b).
There is no specific method by which the municipal clerk needs to do this, 
and many clerks complete it by using one (or more) of the following:
 Ɋ Posting any tally sheets and machine tapes for each reporting unit in the 
municipal clerk’s, or school district clerk’s, office and on the municipality’s 
or school district’s website.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 236
 Ɋ If using the Commission’s Canvass Reporting System (“CRS”), upload or 
enter Election Night results into the CRS, and print the “Canvass Report-
EL-106,” and post in the municipal clerk’s office and on the municipality’s 
website. See Chapter 15.5: “Canvass Reporting System” for more 
information on how to use the CRS.
 Ɋ If not using the CRS to organize returns, enter results into an Excel or Word 
document, and post them in the municipal clerk’s office and on the 
municipality’s website.
Posting Provisional Voter Totals
At the same time, the municipal clerk is also required to post at their office and 
on their website the number of voters who cast provisional ballots who did not 
later appear on Election Day to provide their missing photo identification or their 
Driver’s License or State ID card number. Wis. Stat. § 7.15(15).
Posting Absentee Voter Totals for “Central Count” Jurisdictions
For jurisdictions where absentee ballots are canvassed using 
“central count,” the municipal clerk also must post:
1. The total number of absentee ballots the clerk has sent to voters; and
2. The total number of absentee ballots that were returned. Wis. Stat. § 7.52(1)(c).
The clerk should not post voters’ names and addresses as part of this reporting. Id.
Sec. 14.25.4: Step 4: Municipal 
Clerk Transmits Unofficial Results 
to County Clerk within 2 Hours
The municipal clerk must report the unofficial results, by ward or reporting 
unit, to their county clerk no later than two hours after the votes are tabulated. 
Wis. Stat. § 7.51(4)(c). This does not mean two hours after polls close; it means 
within two hours of when each jurisdiction finishes counting all their ballots. 
Municipal clerks should proof their results again before transmitting them to 
the county clerk to ensure there are no errors or transpositions of numbers.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 237
Note: For jurisdictions that modem their results directly to the county 
clerk’s election management software, separate reporting by the 
municipal clerk is not necessary.
Sec. 14.25.5: Step 5: County Clerks 
Post Unofficial Results within 2 Hours
The county clerk is required to keep their office open on Election Night to post 
unofficial results. Wis. Stat. § 7.60(1). The county clerk must post unofficial results, 
by ward or reporting unit, on their county website no later than two hours after 
receiving them. Id.
Best Practice: This should be done on a rolling basis as results are received.
County clerks then enter the election results into the CRS within nine days of 
a February nonpartisan primary or April general election (with no presidential 
preference vote), within 10 days of the August partisan primary, and within two 
weeks of the November general election. See Chapter 15.5: “Canvass Reporting 
System” for more information on entering results into the CRS.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 238
Chapter 14.26: Election Night 
Duties
After the polls close, state law requires municipal and county clerks to perform 
specific Election Night duties.
Sec. 14.26.1: Election Night 
Duties of Municipal Clerks
Municipal clerks are responsible for several important end-of-night tasks on 
Election Day.
Enter Provisional Ballot Information into WisVote
If a jurisdiction receives zero provisional ballots on Election Day, no action is 
needed for provisional ballots on Election Night. If provisional ballots were issued, 
municipal clerks should input that information into WisVote on Election Night.
For detailed instructions on entering provisional ballot information, see 
Chapter 5.3 of the WisVote manual.
Post Number of Outstanding Provisional Ballots
As soon as possible after the polls close, the municipal clerk must post the 
total number of outstanding provisional ballots at their office and on their 
website. Wis. Stat. § 7.15(15). An “outstanding provisional ballot” is one in which 
the voter did not return to the polling place by 8 p.m. to show acceptable 
proof of photo ID. The clerk also must make this number available to anyone 
who requests it. Id.
Notify other Canvassers of Provisional Ballots
If the municipal clerk has been made aware by any of their poll workers that 
provisional ballots were cast, they should notify the board of canvassers of each 
municipality, county, and school district impacted on Election Night. Wis. Stat. 
§ 6.97(3)(b).
For more information on processing provisional ballots after Election Day, see 
Chapter 15.2: “Rehabilitating Provisional Ballots.”
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 239
Report Returns to County Clerk
Municipal clerks are required to report their returns, by ward or reporting unit, 
to the county clerk no later than 2 hours after the votes are tabulated. Wis. Stat. 
§ 7.51(4)(c).
Note: This step may be completed concurrently with the local board 
of canvassers results reporting if unofficial results are transmitted 
directly to the county clerk via secure modem.
Secure Sensitive Election Materials
Municipal clerks should not leave at the end of Election Night without ensuring that 
sensitive election materials, such as tabulators and ballots, are securely stored.
Sec. 14.26.2: Election Night 
Duties of County Clerks
County clerks also have important end-of-night duties.
Receive & Post Unofficial Results
The county clerk’s office must remain open on Election Night to receive and 
post all unofficial results. Wis. Stat. § 7.60(1). The method by which municipalities 
transmit results to the county will vary, but they are required by Wis. Stat. 
§ 7.51(4)(c) to report the returns, by ward or reporting unit, to the county clerk 
no later than two hours after votes are tabulated. Regardless of the method of 
delivery, results by reporting unit must be posted to the county website no later 
than two hours after receiving them. Wis. Stat. § 7.60(1).
Note: In jurisdictions where unofficial results are transmitted by secure 
modem, county clerks will receive results directly from the local board 
of canvassers (LBOC).
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 240
Best Practices for County Clerk Posting of Unofficial Results: The county 
clerk should develop and document systematic procedures for making 
results public.
The procedures should include a detailed method of proof reading 
which includes double checks by more than one person. This will help 
to ensure that publicized returns, though unofficial, accurately and 
completely, reflect the returns as received from the municipalities.
The county clerk must post returns to their website and may also post 
the results publicly using other methods. See Wis. Stat. § 7.60(1).
Other suggestions for making results public:
 Ɋ Post returns outside of the county clerk’s office or in a media area. 
This can be done by:
 Ɋ Posting tally sheets and machine tapes for each reporting unit.
 Ɋ Printing results from compilation software, if applicable, and posting 
printouts periodically.
 Ɋ Uploading or entering election night results into the Canvass 
Reporting System (CRS) and posting results periodically on the 
county website.
 Ɋ Transferring returns to a continuously updated spreadsheet.
For more information on using the Canvass Reporting System, see the Canvass 
Reporting System Manual.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 241
Chapter 14.27: Municipal 
Boards of Canvassers
The Municipal Board of Canvassers (MBOC) meets after every election to 
canvass all ballots, regardless of whether there are municipal offices or 
referenda on the ballot. Wis. Stat. § 7.53.
Sec. 14.27.1: What Does “Canvass 
the Returns” Mean?
The purpose of the canvass is to determine and certify that the person who 
received the greatest number of votes is declared elected. Wis. Stat. § 5.01(3)(a). 
Though statute does not specifically define the term, many courts have 
confirmed that the “canvass” is “purely ministerial” or “purely administrative.”1 
In other words, the purpose of the MBOC is not to examine the legitimacy 
of the election, it’s to ensure that the unofficial results accurately reflect the 
ballots that were cast by voters. Canvassers cannot delay or wait for a recount 
to perform their duties.2 A majority of the board of canvassers may act to 
complete their duties, even if one member is absent.3
Sec. 14.27.2: When Does 
the MBOC Meet?
The MBOC either meets on Election Night or no later than 9 a.m. on the Monday 
after the election. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(d).
The MBOC is a public meeting, and must be noticed in accordance with 
Wisconsin’s open meetings laws. Wis. Stat. § 19.83(1).
1 State ex rel. Husting v. State Canvassers, 159 Wis. 216 (WI 1914); Hammond v. Bd. Of Canvassers, 2003 
WI APP 22 (Ct. Appeals D3).
2 State ex rel. Husting v. State Canvassers, 159 Wis. 216 (WI 1914).
3 Karker v. Board of Unified School District, 51 Wis. 2d 542 (W 1971).
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 242
Meeting on Election Night
In municipalities with one polling place, one set of results, no provisional 
ballots, and no “central count,” the poll workers become the MBOC on Election 
Night. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(1)(a). They complete the canvass statement, certify the 
municipal election results and officially determine the winners of municipal 
contests. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(1).
Meeting after Election Day
In municipalities with more than one polling place where results must be combined, 
the MBOC may convene as soon as they receive the last results from all polling 
places but no later than 9 a.m. on the Monday after the election. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(d).
What If There are Provisional Ballots?
If there are provisional ballots that were not resolved on Election Day, the MBOC 
cannot be held on Election Night. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(1)(a). In these instances, the 
MBOC will need to reconvene after the election but no later than 9 a.m. the 
Monday after the election. Wis. Stat. §§ 7.53(1)(a), 7.53(2)(d).
Sec. 14.27.3: Who Serves on the MBOC?
The MBOC consists of the municipal clerk and two other qualified voters of the 
municipality appointed by the municipal clerk. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2). An appointed 
clerk who is not a resident of the municipality may serve as a member of the 
Municipal Board of Canvassers. Members of the MBOC serve on two-year terms 
commencing on January 1 of even-numbered years. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(a)1. If the 
clerk’s office is vacant or the clerk cannot perform their MBOC duties, the mayor, 
president, or board chairman appoints another qualified voter to serve in the 
clerk’s place. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(a)2.
If the clerk is a candidate at the election being canvassed, the clerk can still 
serve on the MBOC if they ran unopposed. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(a)3. Otherwise, the 
mayor, president, or board chairman will appoint another qualified voter. Id.
If any other member of the MBOC is a candidate at the election being 
canvassed, the clerk shall appoint another qualified voter to temporarily fill the 
MBOC vacancy for that election. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(2)(a)4.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 243
Sec. 14.27.4: Broadcasting & 
Recording the Meeting
If the MBOC decides to live stream or broadcast their meeting (including 
holding it virtually on Zoom or another video conference platform), it must be 
recorded. Wis. Stat. § 7.53(5). The recording must then be retained for 22 months. 
Wis. Stat. §§ 7.53(5), 7.23(1)(f).
Sec. 14.27.5: Preparing for the 
MBOC on Election Night
For jurisdictions that are able to conduct the MBOC on Election Night, the 
municipal clerk should ensure that the following materials are available at the 
polling place:
 Ɋ Ballot bag or container complete with the Ballot Container Certificate (EL-
101)
 Ɋ Rejected Absentee Ballot (EL-102) bag or container
 Ɋ Used Certificate Envelopes (EL-103) bag or container
 Ɋ Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104)
 Ɋ Tally Sheet (EL-105) for recording votes
 Ɋ Board of Canvassers Statement (EL-106)
Sec. 14.27.6: Preparing for the 
MBOC after Election Day
The following steps are the Commission’s best practices in preparing for and 
conducting the MBOC. Municipal clerks have a large role to play in this process, 
and organizing materials and information ahead of time will make canvassing 
the returns easier.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 244
Step 1: Organize the Materials for Each Reporting Unit
For each reporting unit, organize the end of night materials 
so they are easy for the MBOC to review and search:
 Ɋ Inspectors’ Statements (EL-104) and accompanying documentation
 Ɋ LBOC Statements
 Ɋ Tally Sheets
 Ɋ All other End of Night materials (see Chapter 14.24: “Closing the Polls, 
LBOC, & End of Night Process” on page 219)
Step 2: Organize & Include Provisional Ballot Materials
The municipal clerk also needs to organize and prepare 
provisional ballot materials, including:
 Ɋ Provisional Ballot(s)
 Ɋ EL-108 Inspectors’ Certificate of Provisional Ballots
 Ɋ EL-123 Provisional Certificate Envelope(s)
 Ɋ EL-123R Provisional Ballot Reporting Form
 Ɋ Processing instructions for the MBOC
 Ɋ All other documentation
See “Provisional Voting” on page 95 for more detailed information on issuing 
provisional ballots. For more detailed information on processing provisional 
ballots after Election Day, see Chapter 15.2: “Rehabilitating Provisional Ballots.”
Step 3: Prepare the Canvass Statement (EL-106)
The MBOC will review and complete the Board of Canvassers Report (EL-106), 
but the municipal clerk may assist them by pre-filling it with the necessary 
information ahead of their meeting.
See the top of page 2 of the EL-106 for instructions on the type of information the 
municipal clerk can prepare and enter on the EL-106 for the MBOC’s review.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 245
Step 4: Arrange for Minutes to be Taken at the MBOC
The meeting of the MBOC must be noticed as a public meeting, and must follow 
Wisconsin’s open meetings law, which includes taking minutes of the proceedings. 
See Wis. Stat. § 19.85(1). Prior to the convening of the MBOC, the municipal clerk 
should arrange for someone to take accurate and detailed minutes.
Sec. 14.27.7: Conducting the Canvass
Statute does not provide specific details for what it means to conduct the 
canvass, but this chapter constitutes the Best Practices recommended by the 
Commission. See generally, Wis. Stat. §§ 7.53(1) and (2).
If anything is missing, information is incomplete, or the MBOC must decide 
or take a vote on any aspect of the canvass, all such information should be 
recorded accurately and in detail in the meeting minutes. Notable information 
to record could include a discrepancy between the number of absentee 
ballots sent to the polling place and the number of absentee ballots counted, a 
discrepancy between the total number of votes cast on the voting equipment 
and total number of voters with no explanation on the EL-104, or missing 
provisional ballot documentation.
Step 1: Review the Materials from Each 
Reporting Unit & Assign Tasks
Each reporting unit should have the same set of paperwork and information 
for MBOC review:
 Ɋ One original of each Tally Sheet (EL-105) and one copy of the voting 
machine tape (if any)
 Ɋ One Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104) plus any additional accompanying 
paperwork for absentees, observers, and challenges
 Ɋ One Absentee Ballot Log (EL-124) (if any)
 Ɋ Provisional Ballot Reporting Form (EL-123R)
 Ɋ Statement of the Municipal Clerk (EL-106P)
If something is missing, or the MBOC has questions about this paperwork, the 
municipal clerk should be consulted immediately.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 246
When assigning tasks, build in redundancy or assign tasks to pairs of individuals 
to ensure that returns and materials from all reporting units are inspected, 
reconciled, and the official results are properly documented for certification.
Step 2: Review Poll List (Number of Voters vs. Number of Votes)
The purpose of Step Two is to ensure that the total number of people who cast 
ballots equals the total number of ballots that were counted.
For each poll list (poll book) for each reporting unit, ensure the following:
 Ɋ Verify that the poll list certification has been completed. This will usually 
be printed on the cover page of the poll book.
 ɉ Is the last voter number listed?
 ɉ Is the page on which the last voter number appears indicated?
 ɉ Is the number of absentee voters indicated?
 ɉ Has the number of voters whose provisional ballots were counted 
been added?
 Ɋ Compare the number of ballots cast to the number of voters.
 ɉ If the number of voters is more than the number of ballots cast:
 Ɉ Every effort should be made to discover the reason for the discrepancy. 
For example, one possible reason would be a voter leaving the polling 
place after checking in but before casting their ballot.
 Ɉ Consult the Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104) for an explanation.
 Ɉ Regardless of the explanation (or lack thereof), record discrepancy 
in minutes.
 ɉ If the number of ballots cast is more than the number of voters:
 Ɉ This is a problem that must be further investigated.
 Ɉ Check the Inspectors’ Statement for an explanation.
 Ɉ If there is no explanation, check voter list for erroneously assigned 
voter numbers.
 Ɉ If the discrepancy cannot be determined, contact chief inspector.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 247
 Ɉ If chief inspector has no explanation or explanation is not sufficient, 
the inspectors must be called in to either find the error or explain the 
discrepancy. This may require hand counting the ballots, whether 
Optical Scan or Paper Hand Count ballots, to verify the error is not in 
the number of ballots.
 Ɉ If inspectors cannot find an error and the discrepancy cannot be 
explained, the draw down procedure is conducted by the inspectors. 
Conducting a draw down is always a last resort, and should not be 
done if the discrepancy can be logically explained.
Note: If polling place had more than one reporting unit, and the overall 
total of ballots cast at the polling place is not more than the overall 
number of voters at the polling place, note this in minutes but do not 
draw down (this likely indicates a number of voters were given the 
wrong ballot).
Step 3: Open & Count Provisional Ballots
The municipal clerk notifies the MBOC if there are provisional ballots to be 
counted. Wis. Stat. § 6.97(3)(b). The municipal clerk will also inform the MBOC 
which provisional ballots can be counted and which cannot. Id.
For more detailed information on how municipal clerks process provisional 
ballots after Election Day, see Chapter 15.2: “Rehabilitating Provisional 
Ballots.”
To process provisional ballots, the MBOC should take the following steps and 
record all decisions in the meeting minutes. Id.
 Ɋ Confirm the seal on the ballot bag containing the provisional ballots has 
not been opened
 Ɋ Review the Provisional Ballot Reporting Form (EL-123r)
 ɉ Confirm the provisional ballot was properly issued
 ɉ Confirm that the voter provided the missing information in order to 
have their provisional ballot counted
 Ɋ Remove the voter’s Provisional Ballot Certificate Envelope (EL-123) from the 
Inspectors’ Certificate of Provisional Ballots (EL-108) envelope
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 248
 Ɋ Confirm that the Provisional Ballot Certificate Envelope (EL-123) has not 
been opened or tampered with
 Ɋ Issue the voter a sequential voter number, starting with the last voter 
number issued on Election Day in that specific reporting unit
 Ɋ Remove the voter’s ballot
 ɉ Review the ballot and award votes where voter intent can be 
determined
 ɉ Adjust the Tally Sheet (EL-105) to reflect the updated vote totals
 Ɋ Provide a copy of the EL-123r and updated tally sheet to other affected 
Boards of Canvassers
Step 4: Troubleshoot for Potential Problems
The MBOC should carefully review the results and documentation from reporting 
units for anything that does not make sense or cannot be readily explained.
Example 1: Discrepancy in Turnout vs. Voters
Compare the number of voters to the number of votes cast for the office 
that would have received the largest number of votes (such as President or 
Governor). These numbers should be very similar because all voters presumably 
would have voted in the highest profile races on the ballot. A large discrepancy 
must be investigated and could signal a problem with voting equipment.
Example 2: Discrepancy in Absentee Ballots Returned vs. Absentees Counted
The municipal clerk should provide the total number of absentee ballots that 
were returned in the jurisdiction. This number should be compared with the total 
numbers of absentee voters reported on each inspectors’ statements. If the 
numbers do not match, this could be a sign that not all absentee ballots that 
were received were counted. The MBOC should also examine the poll books for 
any voter with an “Absentee Returned” watermark or other indicator, but with no 
voter number assigned. That could also be a sign that an absentee ballot was 
not counted.
Example 3: Skewed Statistics in Inspectors’ Statements
Also examine the voter statistics section of the Inspector’s Statement for any 
numbers that seem skewed. For example, if there are 100 registered voters in a 
ward, and the poll workers recorded that there were 1,000 absentee voters, this 
is likely a typo that needs to be corrected or investigated further.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 249
Figure 48: Statistics Section on Inspectors’ Statement
Step 5: Review Tally Sheets & Electronic Vote Records
 Ɋ Review Tally Sheets (or write-in Tally Sheets) for accuracy and verify 
math. If there were registered write-in candidates, all the totals for those 
candidates should equal the number that was recorded on the summary 
statement listing out the total votes for that candidate. If poll workers had 
to adjust the results tapes (for example because a ballot jam led to the 
subtraction of one vote), make sure that math is also correct and reflected 
on the actual tally sheet and summary statement.
 Ɋ Where electronic equipment is used, review the tapes for any adjustments 
made on the tapes and verify the math.
 Ɋ If inspectors have added electronic votes and hand-count votes together, 
check the addition.
 Ɋ Be sure any provisional ballot votes have been attributed.
Step 6: Review Inspectors’ Statement & 
Accompanying Documentation
Each Inspectors’ Statement from each reporting unit must be reviewed in detail 
by the MBOC.
What to Look for
Tamper-Evident Seal Records from Voting Equipment.
Verify that the tamper-evident serial numbers from the voting equipment have 
been recorded and initialed by the Chief Poll worker. Serial numbers should be 
verified on at least 5 Inspectors’ Statements or on 10% of Inspectors’ Statements, 
whichever is greater.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 250
Figure 49: Tamper-Evident Seal Records Section on Inspectors’ Statement
Notations on Incident Log
The Incident Log of each Inspectors’ Statement should be reviewed for any 
notations which impact the poll book numbers or number of votes.
Step 7: Compare the Vote Totals to the Tally 
Sheet Prepared by Municipal Clerk
Ahead of the meeting of the MBOC, the municipal clerk will have prepared 
a tabular statement, or some other document showing a summary of each 
reporting unit.
The MBOC should read each candidate’s name and the number of votes they 
received, as documented by the results tapes and/or tally sheets. Each number 
should be compared to the totals on whatever summary documentation the 
municipal clerk provided.
The MBOC should correct any errors as needed. If corrections are made, the other 
canvassers verify the correction. Record any errors and corrections in minutes.
First Edition, June 2026 Election Day 14 – 251
Step 8: Review & Confirm Write-In Votes
Even in places that use electronic voting equipment, write-in votes will still 
always be recorded on tally sheets. From the tally sheets, read the names of 
write-in candidates and the number of votes cast for each. Verify the accuracy 
of inspectors’ hash marks and math. Compare the results to any summary 
statement prepared by the clerk and make any necessary corrections. If 
corrections are made, the other canvassers very the correction. Record any 
errors/corrections in minutes.
See Chapter 14.21: “Write-In Votes” on page 180 for detailed guidance on 
write-in scenarios and counting votes.
Step 9: Complete the Canvass Statement (after 
All Reporting Units have been Reviewed)
When all corrections have been made, print a new tally sheet, summary 
statement and certification. Once the new statements have been printed, the 
board of canvassers then should proof the new materials to verify their accuracy.
Once the canvassers are certain the results are correct as recorded, the 
canvass board members sign the certification.
Sec. 14.27.8: Breaking a Tie 
in a Municipal Contest
If there are any tie votes in municipal offices, the canvassers break the tie. Wis. Stat. 
§ 5.01(4)(a). Statute directs that this be done “by lot,” which can include flipping a 
coin, drawing names out of a hat, or by anything that will determine a random 
result. Id. If the tie occurs during a primary and only one candidate may advance, 
the tie is likewise broken by lots. Id. If all candidates in a tied contest are present, the 
candidates can draw the lots themselves. Wis. Stat. § 5.01(4)(c). If not all candidates 
are present, the MBOC shall break the tie. Id. There cannot be a tie for a referendum 
question — if both “Yes” and “No” receive the equal number of votes, the question 
fails adoption. Wis. Stat. § 5.01(4)(d).
Sec. 14.27.9: Canvass Retention
The canvass statement must be kept for 10 years after the election for which it 
was prepared. Wis. Stat. § 7.23(1)(i).

Instructions for Signing this Certification
…(The) ballots…shall be secured (sealed in this ballot bag) by the chief inspector, and, if available, one other inspector whose party affiliation is 
different from the chief inspector’s party affiliation…Wis. Stat. § 7.51(3)(a).An inspector is “unaffiliated” unless his or her name was submitted as an 
inspector nominee by one of the two dominant parties in the county (generally, the Democratic and Republican Parties). 
1.If all inspectors are unaffiliated, the Chief Inspector (CI) and any other inspector must sign the certification.
2.If there is a mix of unaffiliated and affiliated inspectors:
a.If the CI is affiliated,the CI and an inspector affiliated with the other party must sign this certification, if available.  Otherwise the CI
andan unaffiliated inspector must sign the certification.
b.If the CI is unaffiliated, the CI and an affiliated inspector must sign this certification.  If inspectors from both parties are present, the CI
and one inspector from each party should sign the certification.

This container, secured with a numbered seal with the ballots enclosed, shall be returned immediately to the municipal clerk.  Wis. Stat. § 7.51(3)(a) and (5).  The 
municipal clerk shall provide for the delivery of county, state, federal and school ballots to the appropriate clerk.  §7.51(5), Wis. Stats.  If the municipality has 
designated a central counting location pursuant to Wis. Stat. § 7.51(1), the municipal clerk or two inspectors shall transport the container to the central counting 
location designated by the municipal clerk.  Wis. Stat. § 5.85(5).

Election Inspectors
• Ballots of absentee voters which are rejected pursuant to the provisions of §6.88, Wis. Stats., shall be 
kept in their certificate envelopes and returned enclosed in this carrier envelope to the municipal clerk.
• These ballots may not be counted, and the names of voters may not be entered on the poll list or 
assigned a number on the registration list.
• Each certificate envelope should be numbered and the reason for rejection listed on the back of the 
envelope and on the Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104).
• This carrier envelope will be returned by the inspectors to the municipal clerk after the polls close.  
§6.88(3)(b), Wis. Stats.
Municipal and County Clerks
• The municipal clerk is responsible for delivery of this carrier envelope to the county clerk by 4 p.m. on the 
day following the election if a county, state or federal election is held in conjunction with the election for 
which these ballots were cast.  If there is no county, state or federal election held in conjunction with this 
election, this envelope shall be kept by the municipal clerk or returned to the clerk responsible for 
administering the election for which the absentee ballots were cast.  §7.51(3)(d), (5), Wis. Stats.
• In the event of a recount, the county clerk or appropriate municipal clerk will return this carrier envelope 
to the board of canvassers responsible for conducting the recount.  §9.01, Wis. Stats.

                                       
Election Inspectors or Municipal Board of Absentee Ballot Canvassers (Election Officials)
 Election officials shall announce the name of each absentee elector.
 When the election officials find that the certification has been properly executed, and the absentee voter
is a qualified elector of the ward and has not voted in the election, they shall assign a voter number to
the elector’s name on the poll list and the Absentee Ballot log, and enter an indication on the poll list
that the elector voted absentee.
 The election officials shall open the envelope containing the ballots so as not to deface or destroy the
certification.
 The election officials shall remove the ballots and deposit them in the proper ballot containers.  §§6.88(3)(a),
7.52(3)(a), Wis. Stats.
 Used certificate envelopes of absentee voters who have cast ballots shall be placed in this carrier
envelope and returned to the municipal clerk after the polls close or upon completion of the absentee
ballot canvass.  §§7.51(3)(d), 7.52(4)(i), Wis. Stats.
Municipal and County Clerks 
 The municipal clerk is responsible for delivery of this envelope to the county clerk by 4 p.m. on the day
following the election if a county, state or federal election is held in conjunction with the election for which 
these ballots were cast.  If there is no county, state or federal election held in conjunction with this 
election, this envelope shall be kept by the municipal clerk or returned to the clerk responsible for 
administering the election for which the absentee ballots were cast.  §7.51(3)(d), (5), Wis. Stats. 
 In the event of a recount, the county clerk or appropriate municipal clerk will return this envelope to the
board of canvassers responsible for conducting the recount.  §9.01, Wis. Stats.

registration list, the back of the ballot, the Provisional Ballot Certificate Envelope (EL-123), the Provisional

                                       
Election Inspectors
• Provisional ballots of voters unable to provide the Wisconsin driver license or state identification card number
required by Wis. Stat. §6.55(2)(a)1., and/or unable to provide an acceptable form of photo identification pursuant
to Wis. Stat. § 6.79 (2)(a), shall be kept in their certificate envelopes.
• These ballots may not be counted, unless the voter returns with the required Wisconsin driver license or state
identification card number and/or an acceptable form of photo identification before the polls close.
• The names of provisional voters are assigned a serial number (PV#__) that is recorded on the registration list,
the back of the ballot, the Provisional Ballot Certificate Envelope (EL-123), the Provisional Ballot Reporting
Form (EL-123r), the EL-123is “Provisional Voting InformationSheet” for the electorand listed on the
Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104).
• The back of the ballot should be marked “Section 6.97,” voted by theelector, placed in the completed
provisional ballot certificate envelope and returned to the election inspectors.
• The inspectors make a notation (including the PV#) that the voter was issued a provisional ballot on the
Inspectors’ Statement (EL-104).
• The completed provisional ballot certificate envelopes containing the voted ballots are placed in this carrier
envelope. This carrier envelope and any other provisional ballot carrier envelopes must also be placed in a
separate ballot bag, secured with a tamper-evident seal and labeled with “Provisional Ballots.”
Municipal Clerks
• The municipal clerk shall promptly notify the appropriate boards of canvassers that the clerk has provisional
ballots that may be counted if the voter provides the required Wisconsin driver license or state identification
card number and/or an acceptable form of photo identification by 4:00 p.m. on the Friday following the election.

Wisconsin Election Observers 
Rules-at-a-Glance
This guide presents the main rules 
for election observers. Situations 
may require consulting Wis. Stat. § 
7.41 and Wis. Admin. Code Chapter 
EL 4., but this guide provides what 
most observers need to follow to 
observe an election. 
Members of the public (except 
candidates) may observe 
election activity at polling places, 
municipal clerk offices (that are 
in public buildings) or alternate 
sites, absentee ballot board of 
canvassers sites, central count sites, 
recount sites, and facilities being 
served by Special Voting Deputies.
 
Absentee Ballot Canvasser Sites:  
Observation is allowed during the 
hours an absentee ballot board of 
canvassers is meeting to process 
absentee ballots, starting no later 
than the zeroing of the tabulators. 
Observers may create and transmit 
photos, videos, or audio recordings.
SVD Facilities:  Only one observer 
appointed by each of the two major 
political parties may accompany 
each pair of Special Voting Deputies 
when they are administering absentee 
voting. Observers must comply with 
any visitor requirements of the facility. 
Observers cannot enter a voter’s 
private room, and the voter may opt to 
close the door when voting.
Central Count:  Observation is allowed 
during the hours ballots are tabulated 
at a central counting location. 
Observers may create and transmit 
photos, videos, or audio recordings.
Recounts:  Observation is allowed 
during the hours when recount 
activities are happening. The 
petitioner, opposing candidates, 
interested persons, and associated 
counsel are not observers and not 
subject to these rules. Observers may 
create and transmit photos, videos, or 
audio recordings.
After Voting Activity Ends: 
Candidates may be present, and 
individuals remaining at the location 
may create and transmit photos, 
videos, and audio recordings if such 
activity does not disrupt any election 
administration activity, including the 
canvass meeting at a polling place.
Specific Locations:
Polling Place:  Observation begins 
at the earlier of 7:00 a.m. or when 
election equipment is zeroed out, and 
ends at the later of 8:00 p.m. or when 
the last voter in line has voted. When 
voting concludes, observers may 
remain to view the canvass meeting. 
Photos, videos, and audio recordings 
cannot be created or transmitted until 
the public canvass begins.
Clerk’s Office or Alternate Sites:  
Observation is allowed during 
the hours of in-person absentee 
voting (IPAV) in the clerk’s office (if 
in a public building) or at alternate 
sites. Observers may view the initial 
securing of ballots returned at the 
location during IPAV hours.Wisconsin Elections 
Commission
WEC Helpdesk: 
608-266-8005
WEC Helpdesk e-mail: 
elections@wi.gov
EL-109R December 2025

Observers must:
 ●Notify the designated election 
official that they wish to observe 
 ●Show photo ID with the observer’s 
name, sign the observer log, and 
print their name, address, and any 
organization being represented
 ●Follow the directives established by 
the designated election official
 ●Keep conversation to a minimum
 ●Wear an observer badge or sticker
 ●Depart the location if instructed 
to do so when the number of 
observers representing the same 
organization is limited due to 
space concerns 
 ●Following a warning, cease conduct 
violating the observer rules, and if 
then asked to leave due to violating 
the rules, depart the location. The 
observer will receive a written order 
stating the reason for removal 
before departing.
Observers may:
 ●Observe the public aspects of the 
voting process
 ●Observe from any observer area, 
which must be accessible and 3 to 8 
feet from each table for registration, 
check-in, absentee certificate 
processing, and ballot remaking
 ●Bring a chair, mobility equipment, 
or disability aids, and use available 
chairs and restrooms at the location
 ●Move between observer areas
 ●Observe absentee ballot certificate 
envelopes set aside to be rejected
 ●Ask an election official to repeat, 
once, a name or address
 ●Direct questions to the designated 
election official 
 ●If the observer is an elector, 
challenge a voter for cause under 
Wis. Admin. Code Ch. EL 9.
 ●Take notes, text, and email
 ●Assist a voter who requests the 
observer’s assistance to vote
 ●When not disruptive, view the poll 
list or observer log
Observers may not:
 ●Engage in electioneering, which 
means any activity intended to 
influence voting at an election, 
including displaying or discussing 
anything related to a candidate, 
referenda question, or political party
 ●Post or distribute any election-
related material, or imply that the 
observer is an election official
 ●Engage in loud, boisterous, or 
disruptive behavior, or initiate a 
conversation with a voter
 ●Make audio or visual calls
 ●Record any voted ballots or anyone 
returning or correcting an absentee 
certificate envelope
 ●Create or transmit any photos, 
videos, or audio recordings unless 
expressly permitted (see “Specific 
Locations” section)
 ●See or hear how an elector votes
 ●Handle original election documents 
or view confidential information, 
including personally identifiable 
numbers (birth date, social security, 
bank account etc.) confidential 
voter information, voted ballots, 
registration forms, proof of residence 
documents, and IDs
Communications Media:  Individuals 
who communicate to the designated 
election official that they intend to 
record or report information outside 
of the voting area, and must identify 
themselves and any organization 
represented to the designated election 
official. Video and still camera use is 
allowed outside the voting area only 
at the discretion of the designated 
election official and within the limits 
set by that official, and they may not 
record confidential information or 
disrupt the election. Communications 
media members are not observers 
unless they sign in as an observer.
Polling Place Accessibility 
Assessments:  Certain individuals 
are authorized by the Commission 
to monitor compliance with 
accessibility standards. Accessibility 
Reviewers are not observers.

INELIGIBLE VOTER INFORMATION SHEET
You are being given this informational sheet today because your name and date of birth match a name 
and date of birth on a list of offenders who are currently serving a sentence for a felony conviction.  If 
this information is correct, you are ineligible to vote in Wisconsin and must wait until you have 
completed the terms of your felony sentence including any terms of extended supervision, probation, or 
parole.
• If the information was correct, but you have completed your sentence, have been discharged, or 
your sentence has been vacated, your registration or absentee ballot will be accepted if you can 
provide the poll workers or clerk with a copy of the Discharge Certificate which you received 
from your agent when you were discharged.  Page 2 of the certificate will contain a statement 
that restores your right to vote.  
If you do not have a copy of your discharge certificate, you may obtain one by contacting the 
Department of Corrections (DOC) Master Records Line at (608) 240-3750.  It will take DOC at 
least 24 hours to make a copy of the discharge certificate.  The DOC records office will need the 
address, email, or fax number where a copy of the discharge certificate can be sent.
• If the information on the list is incorrect or you maintain that you are not a felon, you will be 
permitted to register and to vote.  Your registration and any ballot that you cast will be marked as 
“ineligible to vote per Department of Corrections,” until you take steps to resolve the issue with
the Department of Corrections. Your ballot will also be challenged by the election inspectors at 
your polling place.
If you believe that your name and date of birth have been falsely matched or that you may be the 
victim of identity theft, contact the Wisconsin Department of Justice Crime Information Bureau 
(CIB).  CIB provides a process for clarifying a false match.  For assistance with this process 
please call: 
CIB Front Desk (608) 266-7314
Record Check Unit Supervisor (608) 266-9398
After you have resolved the issue contact your municipal clerk and provide them any documentation you 
have confirming that you are not currently serving a sentence for a felony conviction.

PROVISIONAL VOTING INFORMATION SHEET
You are being given a provisional ballot today because one of the following situations applies:
❑ You are unable or unwilling to list your Wisconsin driver license or identification card number
on your voter registration application.
❑ You are unable or unwilling to present an acceptable proof of identification.
Your provisional ballot number is PV#____________________________.
Your provisional ballot will not count unless you provide the poll workers with the required information 
before the polling place closes at 8:00 p.m. tonight, or you provide the required information to the 
municipal clerk by 4:00 p.m. the Friday after the election.
If you need to provide your Wisconsin driver license or identification card number, you may contact the 
municipal clerk using the information below no later than 4:00 p.m. Friday.
If you need to present an acceptable proof of identification, you must present your proof of identification 
to the municipal clerk no later than 4:00 p.m. Friday.  Acceptable forms of proof of identification may 
be found on the back side of this handout.

You may check whether your ballot was counted by conta cting the municipal clerk , checking the 
MyVote WI website at: https://myvote.wi.gov, or by contacting the Wisconsin Elections Commission at 
866-VOTEWIS (868-3947).
NOTE:  Provisional ballots may NOT be issued when a voter is at the wrong polling place or when 
a person is attempting to register in -person at the polling place and does not provide the required 
proof of residence.
Falsification of information on this form is punishable under Wisconsin law as a Class I felony

• A WI Driver License/ID Card, if not expired or canceled; may be used even if driving privileges have been revoked

provided by the university, college or technical college

• (Homeless voters only) A letter from an organization that provides services to the homeless that identifies the voter and 
describes the location designated as the person’s residence for voting purposes 
• A contract/intake document prepared by a residential care facility indicating that the occupant resides in the facility
• A bank/credit card statement 
• A paycheck or pay stub
• A check or other document issued by a unit of government

ID card (Box 7), if applicable, and the proof of residence document you provided in Box 8. 
• Provide your month, day, and year of birth.  
• Providing your phone number and/or email address is optional and is subject to open records requests.  This information 
may be used by your municipal clerk to contact you about your voter record or absentee ballot request. 
• Provide your home address (legal voting residence) in Wisconsin.
• Provide your full street name, including the type (St, Ave, etc.) and any pre- and/or post-directional (N, S, etc.).
• You may not enter a PO Box as a residential address.  A rural route box without a number should not be used.
• A “military elector” is a person, or the spouse or dependent of a person who is a member of a uniformed service or merchant 
marine, a civilian officially attached to a uniformed service and serving outside the United States, or a Peace Corp volunteer.  
Military electors are not required to register as a prerequisite to voting at any election. 
• A “permanent overseas elector” is a US citizen, at least 18 years old, who does not qualify as a resident of this state, but 
who either last lived in this state, or whose parent last lived in this state immediately prior to the parent’s departure from the 
United States, and who is not registered to vote in any other state.
• If your mailing address is different from your home address, provide it here.  A PO Box is acceptable as a mailing address.  
Overseas electors should provide their complete overseas address here.
• Provide full previous name if changed and/or previous address if you have been registered to vote anywhere in the U.S.
• If you have a valid and unexpired WI driver license or WI DOT ID: provide that number.  If you do not know your number, 
please call (608) 266-2353 to get it.
• If you have an expired, canceled, suspended, or revoked WI driver license or WI DOT ID: you must provide the last four 
digits of your Social Security number.  In addition, you may also provide the number on your license or ID (optional). 
• If you have never been issued a WI driver license or WI DOT ID: provide the last four digits of your Social Security number.
• If you do not have a WI driver license or WI DOT ID nor a Social Security Number: please check the appropriate box.
If you are registering to vote on Election Day and have been issued a WI driver license or ID, but are unable or unwilling to 
provide the number, your vote will not be counted unless you provide the number to the election inspectors by 8:00 p.m. on 
Election Day or to your municipal clerk by 4:00 p.m. the Friday following Election Day.  
Assistant:  If you are unable to sign this form due to a physical disability, you may have an assistant do so on your behalf.  That 
assistant must provide his or her signature and address in the space provided.  By signing, the assistant certifies that he or she 
signed the form at your request.

Penalties for Violation.  Whoever swears falsely to any absent elector affidavit under Wis. Stat. § 6.15, may be fined not more than $1,000 or imprisoned 
not more than 6 months, or both.  Whoever intentionally votes more than once in an election may be fined not more than $10,000 or imprisoned not more than 4 years 
and 6 months, or both.
